MRVL — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • One intellectual-property block appears in almost everything it sells: the ultra-high-speed SerDes that moves bits across a package, a board, a cable or a fibre.
  • FY2026 revenue was $8,194.6m: data center $6,100.3m and communications and other $2,094.3m (10-K FY2026, Note 3). In the quarter ended 2026-08-01 the split was $2,171.5m and $567.8m, so data center reached 79% of revenue.
  • Management put custom revenue at approximately $1.5bn for FY2026, "doubled" year over year, on the Q4 FY2026 call (2026-03-05), which is 18.3% of company revenue and 24.6% of the data-center line.
  • Eighteen sockets were disclosed at that date — five XPU and thirteen attach, twelve of them at the top four US hyperscalers — against a pipeline of more than 50 opportunities across more than ten customers worth $75bn of claimed lifetime revenue
  • The usable answer is a range: custom 30–44%, centred near 35–39%; merchant 63–66% `[estimate: mix algebra on disclosed consolidated non-GAAP gross margins and management's custom revenue figures]`.
  • Pure-play design houses set the floor. Alchip's FY2025 gross margin was 26%, up from 20% in 2024, and Global Unichip ran 21.5% in the June 2026 quarter on a turnkey-heavy mix `[web]`. Marvell earns more because it contributes its own SerDes, dense SRAM, die-to-die and packaging blocks instead of only physical design and supply-chain management.
  • Non-recurring engineering receipts are booked as a reduction of operating expense. Koopmans, June 2025: "our customers sort of co-invest and we recognize that as a reduction in our operating expense… the actual investment is actually quite a bit larger than is shown on our P&L." Custom revenue is product revenue only, and custom's operating-margin gap to merchant is narrower than the roughly 26-point gross-margin gap by an amount no filing sizes.
  • The Google warrant, issued 2026-08-18 under a commercial agreement dated 2026-07-29, covers 58,970,907 shares at $206.58: 1,360,867 vest on time and the remaining 57,610,040 vest in 240 equal tranches of 240,042 shares, one per $500m of Custom Products revenue, through the end of FY2033 (8-K filed 2026-08-19).
  • At a 35% pre-warrant custom gross margin, the 5% to 8% band takes the reported margin on Google programs to between about 31% and about 29%, and the realized analogue points to the lower figure.
  • For interconnect the invoice goes to an optical module maker (Innolight, Eoptolink, Coherent, Lumentum, Fabrinet), a cable maker (Amphenol, TE Connectivity, Luxshare) or the ODM building the switch. The hyperscaler's network architecture team specifies the DSP by part number during qualification and then buys the finished module.
  • A module maker qualifies a DSP roughly twelve to eighteen months before volume `[estimate: the realized 1.6T interval, qualification under way by May 2024 against production in the second half of FY2026; the 10-K says only "several months or more"]`, so whoever ships a working part first wins the generation. Marvell was first with 100G-per-lane (800G) and first to productize 200G-per-lane (1.6T), and demonstrated 400G-per-lane at OFC in April 2025.
  • Trade estimates put Marvell at about 60% of the 400G-and-above PAM4 optical DSP market with Broadcom above 30%
  • Peak to trough the franchise went from $3,510.8m in FY2023 to $1,603.1m in FY2025, a 54% decline in two years. FY2026 recovered 31% to a level 40% below the FY2023 peak.
  • The communications gross margin is derived by decomposing reported consolidated non-GAAP gross margin against disclosed end-market mix across three windows, which give 66.3%, 70.5% and 74.6%.
  • The realized driver over that window was the channel inventory cycle, amplified by sell-in accounting on 43% of FY2026 revenue: enterprise networking declined 49% in FY2025 against a campus switch market that contracted 20% in 2024, roughly 2.5x the amplitude of the market it sells into.
competitive-advantage
  • Three or four firms in the world can take a 2.5D or 3D accelerator from architecture to high-volume production at 3nm with in-house 224G SerDes, hardened Arm compute, dense SRAM, custom HBM interfaces and CoWoS-class packaging. Marvell is one of them, and that capability is why hyperscalers keep awarding it sockets. What the capability has not produced is pricing power.
  • The business earns an estimated 30–44% gross margin, centred near 35–39%, against 63–66% for the standard products sold out of the same IP library, and Marvell's reported non-GAAP gross margin has declined in every year since custom production revenue began: 64.5% in FY2023, 61.0% in FY2025, 59.5% in FY2026, 58.9% in each of the first two quarters of FY2027, and a 58.0% midpoint guided for Q3 FY2027.
  • The durability test has already run once and Marvell lost it. Marvell designed Amazon's Trainium 2 and lost the following generation.
  • A design win re-competed roughly every seven quarters is not a switching cost.
  • Marvell issued a warrant over 58,970,907 shares, about 6.7% of the share count, vesting one tranche per $500m of custom-product revenue against purchases the 8-K describes as "discretionary", with no minimum volume. The estimated grant-date fair value is $6bn to $10bn, roughly 5% to 8% of the $120bn it vests against, which takes the reported gross margin on Google programmes from a pre-warrant 35% to about 29%-31%. Broadcom has never issued a customer warrant.
  • Across FY2017 to FY2026 Marvell generated $43,096m of revenue and $27m of cumulative GAAP operating income, with operating losses in five of the ten years. FY2026 was the best year in the company's history and produced a 7.4% return on invested capital, below a fabless leading-edge cost of capital of roughly 10–11%. Broadcom earned 17.0% on the same definition in its latest fiscal year and NVIDIA 71.8%.
  • It earns a position in the middle, toward the lower end: defensible today on execution and IP breadth, open to erosion at every generation boundary, and not yet earning its cost of capital.
  • Alchip Technologies (3661.TW) and Global Unichip (3443.TW) are the closest by the shape of the advantage: the same sole-source-per-programme ASIC design-and-turnkey model, the same customer concentration and the same rebid-per-generation exposure, earning 26% and 21.5% gross margins in their last full fiscal years, and 34.8% and 21.5% in their June-quarter 2026 disclosures.
  • This franchise earns returns above its cost of capital and the source is identifiable: a 224G and 112G SerDes and DSP block that Marvell gets to production earlier than anyone except Broadcom, then reuses across optical DSPs, retimers, AEC DSPs, switch silicon and custom ASICs.
  • On like products Marvell's merchant margin sits below Broadcom, Astera Labs and Credo, and the gap to Broadcom has not closed in five years.
  • Buyer power is the structural limit. Ten customers accounted for 82% of FY2026 revenue, one distributor for 45% of the quarter ended 2026-05-02, and three customers for 75% of gross receivables at that date.
  • Switching costs are per-socket and expire at each speed transition. A module maker that qualifies a Marvell DSP into an 800G transceiver has sunk firmware, thermal, optical-link and interoperability work and will not swap mid-life; the 10-K describes the effect from the incumbent's side ("If a customer's system designer initially chooses a competitor's product, it becomes significantly more difficult for us to sell our products for use in that system"). The lock lasts one product generation. Every eighteen to twenty-four months the socket is re-tendered at the next speed.
  • This franchise holds a real but second-tier position that earns no demonstrable excess return, and the part of it with the strongest evidence of a durable edge has already been sold.
  • Carrier is worse, and the threat is architectural. Revenue went from $1,084.0m in FY2023 to $338.2m in FY2025, and the nine-month FY2026 recovery to $436.3m was about half the equivalent FY2024 period `[web]`. Global RAN equipment sales declined about a fifth between 2022 and 2024 and are forecast to compound at 1% a year to 2030 ([delloro.com, accessed 2026-08-28](https://www.delloro.com/news/ran-market-stabilized-in-2025/)) `[web]`, so Marvell's 69% decline ran at roughly 3.1x the market.
competitive-landscape
  • Marvell is the second-largest full-service custom silicon supplier by revenue and runs at a small fraction of Broadcom's. Counterpoint Research puts Broadcom at 60% and Marvell at 8% of 2027 AI-server compute-ASIC design-partner shipments ([counterpointresearch.com, accessed 2026-08-28](https://www.counterpointresearch.com/insight/ai-server-asic-market)) `[web]`. Marvell's own target is 20% of a $55bn custom compute market by calendar 2028.
  • Per-generation re-bidding: Alchip did the back end for Trainium 1, lost the Trainium 2 generation to Marvell, and regained Trainium 3 on N3 with mass production from late May 2026 `[web]`. Deliberate multi-sourcing: Google now runs Broadcom, MediaTek and Marvell in parallel. And no purchase commitment in the largest disclosed contract: the Google warrant vests against "discretionary purchases", so the $120bn implied by 240 tranches of $500m is a vesting ceiling, not an order.
  • Marvell leads the field on the breadth of the attach portfolio: it defined the category, holds above fifteen attach wins including five CXL sockets across two Tier-1 US hyperscalers, and has line of sight to above $2bn from NIC and CXL alone by FY2029. No design-service house competes there.
  • Against that, $10bn of FY2029 custom revenue is roughly 8–10% share, close to Counterpoint's figure and under half the company target. Marvell can hit the dollar target and miss the share target by more than half.
  • Google TPU v7e into risk production by end-Q1 2026, v8e ordered, a $2bn AI ASIC revenue target for calendar 2026 doubled from $1bn three months earlier, a 10–15% 2027 share target, and $5bn committed to the AI data center business `[web]`. MediaTek's 2026 target is roughly the size of Marvell's whole FY2027 custom business.
  • Marvell is the scale leader in high-speed optical DSPs and pluggable coherent modules and a distant challenger in the other three lines inside this segment.
  • Marvell is the third entrant into a copper market Credo defined and Astera industrialised: Credo did $1.3bn and Astera is running above $1.5bn annualized while Marvell's combined AEC and retimer revenue has never been sized.
  • Linear pluggable optics removes the DSP from the module and leaves Marvell selling the TIA and driver, which it also makes, at lower dollar content. Co-packaged optics moves the photonics onto the switch or accelerator substrate, where the switch vendor specifies who supplies it.
critical-points
  • Custom silicon converts revenue to gross profit at an estimated 30–44% against 63–66% for merchant, so each point of mix moved from merchant to custom costs roughly a quarter-point of consolidated gross margin, and management's own FY2029 custom target moves that mix by about twenty points.
  • FY2026 non-GAAP operating margin of 35.3% is statistically the same as FY2023's 35.5%, on revenue 38% higher. Inside that, non-GAAP gross margin gave up 5.0 points and non-GAAP operating expense declined 4.8 points of revenue.
  • Against a base of roughly 876m shares outstanding at 2026-08-01, the disclosed potential dilution stack is 124.6m shares, or 14.2%.
  • Data center is guided to grow about 60% in FY2027 with interconnect above 70% and custom's written guide at "more than 20%", so roughly 88% to 92% of the FY2027 data-center dollar increase comes from merchant lines
expectations
  • Through FY2028 there is no disagreement to describe: consensus has copied the guide. The disagreement is FY2029 and beyond, where nobody has guided and where the street models revenue accelerating without margin decay.
  • Against the 2018, 2020 and 2021 long-term models, revenue targets were met or beaten every time; gross-margin targets were missed every time and by a widening amount - 4.5 to 6.5 points against the 2021 model - and the free-cash-flow target was missed in every year since FY2020.
forward-model
  • Custom goes from 18.3% of FY2026 revenue to 33.6% of FY2029 revenue and 35.1% by FY2033. At a 38% custom gross margin against 63%–65% in interconnect, every point of custom mix costs roughly 0.26 points of blended gross margin, and adding roughly half a point a year of price erosion in interconnect and switching takes the blend from 59.5% to 54.3% by FY2029 and 52.3% by FY2033.
  • Interconnect dominates because it is the largest revenue line and carries a high margin. At 64.5% in FY2027 it is not the highest — merchant Ethernet switching and communications are both modelled at 67.0% — but it is much the largest, so an eight-point move in its growth rate compounds over six years into a swing roughly the size of the whole of FY2026 revenue.
history-inflections
  • The gross-margin target has been dropped while the operating-margin target has been kept: October 2018 promised above 66% and October 2021 promised 64–66%, and the 38–40% operating margin from the same 2021 model is still guided to, now expected in Q4 FY2027, six years after it was set.
m-and-a
  • And no goodwill impairment has ever been taken against $11,062m of goodwill at 2026-01-31 or $13,874m at 2026-08-01, which under Marvell's single-reporting-unit test would require the whole company's market capitalization to fall below its book value.
management-claims
  • Twenty percent of the $94bn CY2028 data-center market is $18.8bn of FY2029 data-center revenue, and management confirmed the mapping on the Q1 FY2027 call. Subtracting the $10bn-plus custom target leaves $8.8bn for interconnect, switching and storage in FY2029, against $11.1bn–$11.6bn implied for FY2028 by management's own guides.
  • The CY2028 data-center market went from $75bn (April 2024) to $94bn (June 2025) with the CY2023 base of $21bn unchanged and the same source list, moving the compound rate from 29% to 35%; compute was restated up 29% and interconnect 37%.
management-incentives
  • FY2026 was the one year the count declined, from 866.0m to 847.3m, and that decline lasted ten days: 26.6m shares repurchased in FY2026 at an average $76.46, then 24.5m issued for Celestial AI at an implied $78.73 and 2.1m for XConn at $80.43, in the first ten days of FY2027.
secular-trends
  • The October 2021 investor day forecast Marvell's served market at $20bn in CY2021 growing to $30bn in CY2024, a 13% CAGR, and set a 15–20% long-term revenue CAGR target. Marvell's revenue over exactly that window compounded at 7.5% on the company's own pro-forma FY2022 basis of $4,638m, and 8.9% as reported, to $5,767m in FY2025.
  • Marvell's data-center revenue grew 46% in the year hyperscaler capex grew about 80%, and is guided to grow about 60% in a year capex grows 77%.
  • The residual, which contains the PAM4 DSP franchise management calls market-leading, grew about 32% in the year LightCounting puts Ethernet optical transceiver sales up 82% ([lightcounting.com, accessed 2026-08-28](https://www.lightcounting.com/newsletter/en/april-2026-market-forecast-379)) `[web]`. Marvell either lost DSP share, or sold into a mix whose dollar content per module declined, or both.
  • Co-packaged optics moves the optics onto the switch or accelerator package and removes the pluggable DSP socket where Marvell's largest revenue line is booked.
technology
  • This is the one Marvell technology that survives every outcome in the pluggable-versus-co-packaged debate: DSP-based pluggables need it, linear pluggable optics chipsets are "optimized TIAs and laser drivers" (10-K FY2026, Item 1), near-package optics needs it, and linear-drive co-packaged optics needs it.
  • Marvell's differentiation is narrow and deep. It is defensible where the product is a standalone high-speed mixed-signal part sold into a socket Marvell defines. It is weak where the product is a large digital chip competing against an incumbent with more scale — the same 224G SerDes yields roughly 60% share in PAM4 DSPs and roughly 4% in switch silicon.
value-chain
  • Marvell earns a lower gross margin than the foundry that fabricates its chips. On the non-GAAP measure it is about nine points below both TSMC and Broadcom, sixteen below NVIDIA, seventeen below Astera Labs and twenty-six below Micron.
  • Combined trailing-twelve-month infrastructure capital expenditure at Amazon, Alphabet, Microsoft and Meta was $506.7bn. Alphabet discloses that about 60% of its technical infrastructure assets were servers and network equipment at 2025-12-31; applying that share to all four gives a silicon-bearing base of roughly $304bn `[estimate: Alphabet's disclosed 60% server share applied to the four buyers' total capex]`. Marvell's data-center revenue for the twelve months to 2026-08-01 was $7,173m, which is 1.4% of the total capex figure and 2.4% of the server-and-network slice.
  • Advanced packaging and substrate bind before wafers, and memory has become a second binding point.
  • Marvell buys firm capacity for the next four quarters and declines to commit firm volume beyond that.

One intellectual-property block appears in almost everything it sells: the ultra-high-speed SerDes that moves bits across a package, a board, a cable or a fibre.

Peer: Broadcom Inc. (AVGO)

Pure-play design houses set the floor. Alchip's FY2025 gross margin was 26%, up from 20% in 2024, and Global Unichip ran 21.5% in the June 2026 quarter on a turnkey-heavy mix `[web]`. Marvell earns more because it contributes its own SerDes, dense SRAM, die-to-die and packaging blocks instead of only physical design and supply-chain management.

A module maker qualifies a DSP roughly twelve to eighteen months before volume `[estimate: the realized 1.6T interval, qualification under way by May 2024 against production in the second half of FY2026; the 10-K says only "several months or more"]`, so whoever ships a working part first wins the generation. Marvell was first with 100G-per-lane (800G) and first to productize 200G-per-lane (1.6T), and demonstrated 400G-per-lane at OFC in April 2025.

Peer: Lattice Semiconductor Corporation (LSCC)

filingsupports
Revenue recognition standards require recognition of revenue based on estimates and may require us to record revenue from distributors that is in excess of actual end customer demand. Because we have limited ability to forecast inventory levels of our end customers, we depend on the timeliness and accuracy of resale reports from our distributors. Late or inaccurate resale reports could reduce the quality or timeliness of information available to us for forecasting channel inventory and end-customer demand, mask significant build-up of inventories in our distribution channel, and impact our ability to accurately forecast future sales. An inventory build-up in our distribution channel could result in a slowdown in orders, requests for returns from customers, or requests to move out planned shipments.

Marvell issued a warrant over 58,970,907 shares, about 6.7% of the share count, vesting one tranche per $500m of custom-product revenue against purchases the 8-K describes as "discretionary", with no minimum volume. The estimated grant-date fair value is $6bn to $10bn, roughly 5% to 8% of the $120bn it vests against, which takes the reported gross margin on Google programmes from a pre-warrant 35% to about 29%-31%. Broadcom has never issued a customer warrant.

(the “Company”) and Google LLC (“Google”) entered into a commercial agreement relating to the Company’s development of custom semiconductor products to Google (the “Custom Products”). The expanded partnership spans a comprehensive range of custom silicon programs that attach to the TPU ecosystem, including AI inference accelerators, storage controllers, network interface controllers, memory interface controllers, and near-memory compute. Switching costs are per-socket and expire at each speed transition. A module maker that qualifies a Marvell DSP into an 800G transceiver has sunk firmware, thermal, optical-link and interoperability work and will not swap mid-life; the 10-K describes the effect from the incumbent's side ("If a customer's system designer initially chooses a competitor's product, it becomes significantly more difficult for us to sell our products for use in that system"). The lock lasts one product generation. Every eighteen to twenty-four months the socket is re-tendered at the next speed.

One of our primary focuses is on winning competitive bid selection processes, known as “design wins,” to develop products for use in our customers’ products. We devote significant time and resources in working with our customers’ system designers to understand their future needs and to provide products that we believe will meet those needs and these bid selection processes can be lengthy. If a customer’s system designer initially chooses a competitor’s product, it becomes significantly more difficult for us to sell our products for use in that system because changing suppliers can involve significant cost, time, effort and risk for our customers. Thus, our failure to win a competitive bid can result in our foregoing revenues from a given customer’s product line for the life of that product.

Linear pluggable optics removes the DSP from the module and leaves Marvell selling the TIA and driver, which it also makes, at lower dollar content. Co-packaged optics moves the photonics onto the switch or accelerator substrate, where the switch vendor specifies who supplies it.

Peer: IEEE Electronics Packaging Society

websupports ✦ best
LPO systems (Fig. 2) are characterized by high-linearity Transimpedance Amplifiers (TIAs) and the absence of power-hungry Digital Signal Processors (DSPs) / Clock Data Recovery (CDR) in the system. Instead, the signal regeneration and signal equalization that are typically performed by the DSP are split between the switch ASIC, the driver IC and the TIA. Some of the key proponents of LPO in the industry are Macom, Semtech and Maxlinear.
— Sujit Ramachandra (Celestial AI) and Farnood Rezaie (Cisco Systems Inc.), Linear Pluggable Optics - An Overview, IEEE Electronics Packaging Society eNews, 2026-03-01
websupports
By design, LPO offers a scalable path to reconciling high data rates with low power consumption for pluggable modules, while CPO enables direct integration of photonics onto the switch IC, thereby eliminating the need for a standalone module. Although CPO is becoming increasingly popular, LPO is seen as a natural evolutionary path for pluggables, offering lower risk compared to CPO, especially in terms of reliability.
— Sujit Ramachandra (Celestial AI) and Farnood Rezaie (Cisco Systems Inc.), Linear Pluggable Optics - An Overview, IEEE Electronics Packaging Society eNews, 2026-03-01
websupports
Marvell Inc., traditionally known for their DSPs have also announced the availability of a 200G/lane TIA and laser driver chipset that enable 800 Gbps and 1.6 Tbps LPO solutions, aimed at addressing next generation XPU compute fabric networks [8].
— Sujit Ramachandra (Celestial AI) and Farnood Rezaie (Cisco Systems Inc.), Linear Pluggable Optics - An Overview, IEEE Electronics Packaging Society eNews, 2026-03-01

Custom goes from 18.3% of FY2026 revenue to 33.6% of FY2029 revenue and 35.1% by FY2033. At a 38% custom gross margin against 63%–65% in interconnect, every point of custom mix costs roughly 0.26 points of blended gross margin, and adding roughly half a point a year of price erosion in interconnect and switching takes the blend from 59.5% to 54.3% by FY2029 and 52.3% by FY2033.

Peer: IEEE Electronics Packaging Society (authors: Celestial AI, Cisco Systems)

websupports
LPO systems (Fig. 2) are characterized by high-linearity Transimpedance Amplifiers (TIAs) and the absence of power-hungry Digital Signal Processors (DSPs) / Clock Data Recovery (CDR) in the system. Instead, the signal regeneration and signal equalization that are typically performed by the DSP are split between the switch ASIC, the driver IC and the TIA.
— Sujit Ramachandra (Celestial AI) and Farnood Rezaie (Cisco Systems Inc.), Linear Pluggable Optics - An Overview (IEEE Electronics Packaging Society), 2026-03-01
webqualifies
Some of the key proponents of LPO in the industry are Macom, Semtech and Maxlinear. The main advantages offered by LPO are reduced power consumption and lower system latency due to the absence of the DSP and reducing the operational costs.
— Sujit Ramachandra (Celestial AI) and Farnood Rezaie (Cisco Systems Inc.), Linear Pluggable Optics - An Overview (IEEE Electronics Packaging Society), 2026-03-01

Peer: MACOM Technology Solutions Holdings, Inc.

filingqualifies
We provide a complete product portfolio of Transimpedance Amplifier (TIAs), Modulator Drivers, Lasers and Photodetectors, to support single-mode, multi-mode and silicon photonics based transceivers and, in some cases, individual component designs are optimized for use together as a chipset.
— MACOM Technology Solutions Holdings, Inc. Form 10-K for fiscal 2025 (year ended October 3, 2025), 2025-11-14

Marvell's differentiation is narrow and deep. It is defensible where the product is a standalone high-speed mixed-signal part sold into a socket Marvell defines. It is weak where the product is a large digital chip competing against an incumbent with more scale — the same 224G SerDes yields roughly 60% share in PAM4 DSPs and roughly 4% in switch silicon.

During the first and second quarters of fiscal 2025, the Company worked with its foundry and test and assembly suppliers to amend certain manufacturing supply capacity reservation agreements, which resulted in reducing the Company's related purchase level commitments. The Company currently estimates that it has agreed to purchase level commitments of at least $544.6 million of wafers, substrates, and other manufacturing products for fiscal 2026 through fiscal 2033 under the capacity reservation agreements.
— Marvell Technology, Inc. Form 10-K for fiscal 2025, 2025-03-06
filingsupports
We typically place firm orders with our suppliers up to 26 weeks prior to the anticipated delivery to our customer and may make further supply commitments up to 52 weeks to secure capacity. Occasionally, orders may be placed in advance of receiving a binding order from our customers. To secure capacity over the long term, we have entered into and expect to continue to enter into capacity reservation arrangements with certain foundries and substrate partners. We often maintain substantial inventories to meet short lead time orders for multi-year product runs.
— Marvell Technology, Inc. Form 10-K for fiscal 2026, 2026-03-05
filingsupports
Under these arrangements, the Company agreed to pay capacity fees or refundable deposits to the suppliers in exchange for reserved manufacturing production capacity over the term of the agreements, which ranges from 4 to 10 years. In addition, the Company committed to certain purchase levels that were in line with the capacity reserved. The Company currently estimates that it has agreed to purchase level commitments of at least $458.2 million of wafers, substrates, and other manufacturing products for fiscal 2027 through fiscal 2033 under the capacity reservation agreements.
